The host side is `ui.RichClipboardHost` (`ClipboardKind` system or primary, a `ClipboardContent`, a format), which the engine implements with `platform.Clipboard.SetContent` and `GetContent`; a dispatcher or a compositor without it fails with `ClipboardUnavailable` or `ClipboardPrimaryUnavailable`, except for the text of the system clipboard. Wayland: `zwp_primary_selection_device_manager_v1` next to `wl_data_device`, one `selection` per kind (offers, sources and pipes on goroutines); X11: `CLIPBOARD` and `PRIMARY` with `INCR` for every format, and the limit is 64 MB for the content. `EditableText` puts its selected text on the primary selection (once per frame, not when the text is obscured or the selection is a caret) and `SelectableRegion` does it when the selection is final, only where `TargetPlatform` is Linux; a middle click (`TextSelectionGestureDetectorBuilder.OnTertiaryTapUp`) puts the caret at the pointer and pastes it in a field that is not read-only. Not done: the pasting of images and files into a widget (the API reads them), and drag and drop (step 14.6) | Flutter has the system clipboard with text only; Linux desktops paste the selection with a middle click |
| `SystemNavigator` and `SystemSound` call the platform channels | structs of hooks owned by the binding: `SystemNavigator.OnPop` (set to end the app), `OnSetFrameworkHandlesBack` (a no-op except on the Android target platform; the binding sets it to `PlatformDispatcher.SetFrameworkHandlesBack`), `OnSelectSingleEntryHistory`, `OnSelectMultiEntryHistory` and `OnRouteInformationUpdated` (for a host with a browser history, or a test), and `SystemSound.OnPlay` (the desktop has no sounds; a test sets it); `SystemNavigator.Pop(animated ...bool)` takes `animated` as an optional value; the deprecated `location` parameter of `routeInformationUpdated` is not ported | no channels |
| `ServicesBinding.handleKeyEvent` passes the events that no handler or focus node took to the text input | the binding gives the key data that no handler took to `TextInput.HandleUnhandledKey` (step 11.4): a key with a character types it, Enter is the action of the field (or a new line), and keys held with Control, Alt or Meta type nothing | the text goes through the input method first (SPEC §8.6), so the text input takes only what it did not |
| `PointerScrollEvent` axes flip when a key of `ScrollBehavior.pointerAxisModifiers` is down, for a mouse only | the same; the pseudo-keys that `Tester.SendKeyDownEvent` gets (`Shift`, `Control`, `Alt`, `Meta`) send the left key, as the key maps of Flutter's test simulation do | tests only |
| `FocusNode` and `FocusScopeNode` are classes you can extend (`class MyNode extends FocusNode`), and `FocusManager.instance` is a singleton | `FocusNode` is a struct that you configure with `FocusNodeOpts` and callbacks (`SetOnKeyEvent`); `FocusScopeNode` embeds it and `AsScope()` is Dart's `node is FocusScopeNode`; a traversal group's node is a `FocusNode` with the group data. The manager is `WidgetsBinding.FocusManager()` (or `BuildOwner.FocusManager()`), and nodes keep a pointer to it, so `FocusManager.instance` and the focus manager test cases that subclass the node are not ported. The microtask that applies the changes is a function posted to the binding (`Tester.Pump` and `FlushPosted` run it) | no singletons (§12.1) and no inheritance |
| the deprecated `onKey` (`RawKeyEvent`), `RawKeyboard`, `RawKeyboardListener` and the `KeyMessage` handler of `FocusManager` (`KeyMessage` has the events of one key chord) | only `onKeyEvent`; the manager's key handler is `HardwareKeyboard.SetKeyMessageHandler` and works with a single `KeyEvent` (Glutter's embedders send one event per key). `Focus.onKey` and the web-only semantics action listener are not ported, nor the cases that use them | Glutter has no raw key events and no web |
| `DirectionalFocusTraversalPolicyMixin` and the `FocusTraversalPolicy` class that policies extend | `FocusTraversalPolicy` is an interface and `FocusTraversalPolicyBase` is embedded for the shared code (`InitFocusTraversalPolicyBase(&p.FocusTraversalPolicyBase, p, FocusTraversalPolicyBaseOpts{RequestFocusCallback, Directional})`); the mixin is the `Directional` option, which the three built-in policies set. `SortDescendants` takes and returns slices | no inheritance or mixins in Go |
| `FocusNode.dispose` of a node that was made a child of a scope with `setFirstFocus` but never attached by a widget leaves the node in the tree | the node leaves the focus tree when it is disposed, so the manager never notifies a disposed node (`// Deviation:` in `FocusNode.Dispose`); a route that is removed in the frame where it was added needs it | Go's `ChangeNotifier` asserts that it is not used after `Dispose` |
| the focus actions and Intents (`NextFocusIntent`, `RequestFocusIntent`, `DirectionalFocusIntent`, `NextFocusAction` and the others) and `FocusableActionDetector` | they exist since step 10.6, with `Shortcuts`, `Actions` and `WidgetsApp`: the actions are `ContextAction`s except `RequestFocusAction`, and invoked without a context or without a primary focus they panic with the tag `FocusActionWithoutFocus` (Dart's null check). The cases of `focus_traversal_test.dart` that press Tab or the arrow keys send the keys to a `WidgetsApp`. The text field case is covered by the tests of `EditableText` (step 11.4). Not ported: the two `RawKeyboardListener` ones (the deprecated raw keyboard) | the Intents need `Actions` |
| `FocusTraversalGroup` creates its node in `initState` and the policy of the widget can be null | the same (a nil `Policy` means `ReadingOrderTraversalPolicy`); `FocusTraversalGroupOpts` has `DescendantsAreNotFocusable` and `DescendantsAreNotTraversable` because the Flutter defaults are true (§12.2). The geometry tests compare `float32` matrix results with a tolerance of 1e-4 (`ui.Matrix4` is `float32`, §12.10 above) | the options rule |
| `Intent`, `Action<T extends Intent>`, `Map<Type, Action<Intent>>` and `Intent.doNothing` | `Intent` is an interface that a value struct satisfies by embedding `IntentBase` (comparable, so it works as a value in a `ShortcutMap`); `Action` is a non-generic interface with `IntentType() reflect.Type` (an interface type accepts every intent that implements it, which stands for Dart's covariance), embedding `ActionBase` (`InitActionBase(&a.ActionBase, a)` gives it the outer value, so the default methods call the overrides); `ActionMap` is `map[reflect.Type]Action` with keys from `IntentTypeOf[T]()`; `CallbackAction[T]` keeps the type parameter; `Intent.doNothing` is `DoNothingIntent{}`. An action listener is removed by the handle that `AddActionListener` returns. In debug builds an action given an intent it can not handle panics with the tag `ActionIntentMismatch` (Dart: a cast error or the "cannot be handled by" assertion) | Go has no covariance or subclassing of generic classes |
| `ActionDispatcher` and `ContextAction<T>` | `ActionDispatcher` is an interface with `ActionDispatcherBase` (`NewActionDispatcher(focusManager...)`, `InitActionDispatcherBase` so that `InvokeActionIfEnabled` calls an overridden `InvokeAction`); `InvokeAction(action, intent, bc)` takes a nil context for "the context of the primary focus". `ContextAction` is the interface `IsEnabledWithContext`/`InvokeWithContext` that `ContextActionBase` implements, and `NextFocusAction`, `PreviousFocusAction`, `DirectionalFocusAction`, `ScrollAction` and `PrioritizedAction` are context actions | Go has no optional parameters |
| `Action<T>.overridable(defaultAction:, context:)` with `_OverridableAction` and `_OverridableContextAction` | `NewOverridableAction(defaultAction, bc)` is one implementation of both; a default action that is a `ContextAction` makes a context action. The recursion guards are debug-only checks with the tag `ActionOverrideRecursion` (Dart: `debugAssertIsEnabledMutuallyRecursive` and the others), an override that finds itself panics with `ActionOverrideSelf`, and a context action invoked without a context with `ActionContextMissing` | one type covers both Dart classes |
| `Map<ShortcutActivator, Intent>` (`Shortcuts.shortcuts`, `ShortcutManager.shortcuts`, `WidgetsApp.shortcuts`) | `ShortcutMap` is an ordered slice of `ShortcutEntry{Activator, Intent}`: the order decides which of the activators that accept an event wins (Dart's maps keep insertion order too), an activator that appears twice keeps the place of its first entry and the intent of its last, and maps are equal when their normalized entries are. `SingleActivator`, `CharacterActivator` and `LogicalKeySet` are comparable values (a `LogicalKeySet` has at most four keys, encoded sorted; its constructors panic with `KeySetDuplicateKey` and `KeySetEmpty`; `SingleActivator` with a modifier as its trigger panics with `SingleActivatorTriggerIsModifier`). `ShortcutActivator.isActivatedBy` (deprecated) is not ported: call `Accepts` | Go maps can not hold interface keys that are not comparable, and need no order |
| `ShortcutManager`, `Shortcuts.manager` and `ShortcutRegistry` | `ShortcutManager` is an interface with `ShortcutManagerBase` (`InitShortcutManagerBase`), `NewShortcutsManager(manager, child)` is `Shortcuts.manager`; `ShortcutsOpts.ExcludeSemantics` is the negation of `includeSemantics`. The menu serialization (`ShortcutSerialization`, `ShortcutManager.serializeForMenu` and the `SingleActivator`/`CharacterActivator` serialization) is not ported: it exists for the platform menu bar, which Glutter does not have yet. `ShortcutRegistry` takes the scheduler for its post-frame notification (`NewShortcutRegistry(binding.Scheduler())`) and its entry errors have the tags `ShortcutRegistryEmpty`, `ShortcutRegistryDuplicate`, `ShortcutRegistryEntryDisposed` and `ShortcutRegistryEntryForeign` | no singletons; the menu bar comes later |
| `DefaultTextEditingShortcuts`, `intentForMacOSSelector` and the text editing intents | `DefaultTextEditingShortcuts` and `DefaultTextEditingShortcutMap()` have only the Linux bindings (common, clipboard, numpad with Num Lock, Home and End), and every target platform uses them (SPEC §11.1: "Linux bindings"); the Apple, Windows and Android maps, the web disabling maps and `intentForMacOSSelector` are not ported. The directional intents are values with constructors (`NewDeleteCharacterIntent(forward)`, `NewExtendSelectionToLineBreakIntent(forward, collapse, opts)`, ...); `collapseSelection` together with `collapseAtReversal` panics with the tag `CaretMovementIntentInvalid`. `ReplaceTextIntent` and `UpdateSelectionIntent` exist since step 11.4. `SelectionChangedCause` is in `services` with `SelectionChangedCauseUnspecified` as its zero value | Glutter is Linux first |
| `WidgetsApp` and `WidgetsApp.router` | (a `TapRegionSurface` is in the tree of both since step 11.4) `NewWidgetsApp(color, opts)` and the generic `NewWidgetsAppRouter[T](color, opts)` (the router configuration type `T` replaces `Router<Object>`); `WidgetsApp.defaultShortcuts` and `defaultActions` are the functions `WidgetsAppDefaultShortcuts()` and `WidgetsAppDefaultActions()` (a new map each call, no mutable global; the web shortcuts are not ported). `LocaleListResolutionCallback` and `LocaleResolutionCallback` return a second `bool` for Dart's null; `PageRouteFactory` is not generic; `LocalizationsResolver` takes the binding (`NewLocalizationsResolver(binding, supported, opts)`) and compares the supported locales by value. The debug assertions and the thrown `FlutterError`s are the tags `WidgetsAppHomeRedundant`, `WidgetsAppHomeAndDefaultRoute`, `WidgetsAppNoRoute`, `WidgetsAppBuilderOnly`, `WidgetsAppPageRouteBuilderMissing`, `WidgetsAppRouterConfigConflict`, `WidgetsAppRouterDelegateMissing`, `WidgetsAppRouterParserMissing`, `WidgetsAppUnknownRouteMissing`, `WidgetsAppUnknownRouteNil`, and a locale that some delegate does not support is reported once with `LocaleNotSupported`. Not part of it yet: `RootRestorationScope` (`restorationScopeId`), `SharedAppData`, `WindowManager`, `PerformanceOverlay`, `SemanticsDebugger`, `WidgetInspector`; the `CheckedModeBanner` is in it since step 12.4 (`WidgetsAppOpts.DebugShowCheckedModeBanner`, an `Opt[bool]` that is true by default and counts only in debug builds; `WidgetsApp.debugAllowBannerOverride` is not ported as it is a global flag); `useInheritedMediaQuery` (deprecated) is dropped. The `Focus` that dismisses the tooltips on Escape is in it since step 11.5 | each is added with the widget it needs |
| `FocusableActionDetector` | `FocusableActionDetectorOpts` names the negated defaults: `Disabled` (`enabled`), `DescendantsAreNotFocusable`, `DescendantsAreNotTraversable` and `ExcludeFocusSemantics` (`includeFocusSemantics`). A highlight callback that would run during the build phase panics with the tag `FocusableActionDetectorCallbackDuringBuild` (Dart: an assertion) | SPEC §12 defaults |

| `TextInput` is a singleton with static methods, and talks to the engine over the `flutter/textinput` channel with JSON (`TextInput.setClient`, `setEditingState`, `TextInputClient.updateEditingState` and the rest); `toJSON`/`fromJSON` of `TextEditingValue`, `TextInputConfiguration`, `AutofillConfiguration` and `TextEditingDelta` | `services.TextInput` is a value that the `WidgetsBinding` owns (`Binding.TextInput()`) and `ui.PlatformDispatcher` is its backend: the platform control (Flutter's `_PlatformTextInputControl`) gives the platform a `ui.TextInputState` (enabled, view, purpose, hints, surrounding text without the composing text, selection, caret rectangle in view coordinates) through `PlatformDispatcher.SetTextInput`, and takes `ui.TextInputEvent`s (delete, commit, preedit, in that order) from `PlatformDispatcher.OnTextInput`, applying them to its copy of the value as Flutter's engines do and giving the client a value or a `TextEditingDelta`. The messages of the channel are methods `HandleUpdateEditingState(clientID, value)`, `HandlePerformAction` and so on; a message for another client id is dropped, and -1 is accepted in a debug build. There is no JSON; `NewTextEditingDeltaFromReplacement` is `TextEditingDelta.fromJSON` without the map | no channel and no singleton (§13); the platform keeps no model of the text |
| `TextEditingValue()` has `selection` collapsed at -1 and `composing` `TextRange.empty` as defaults; `TextInputConfiguration` has non-null defaults; `TextInputAction.unspecified` is a value | `NewTextEditingValue(TextEditingValueOpts)` (the Go zero value has a caret at 0) and `TextEditingValueEmpty`; `NewTextInputConfiguration(opts)` with the negated option names `DisableAutocorrect`, `DisableSuggestions`, `DisableInteractiveSelection` and `DisableIMEPersonalizedLearning`; `TextInputActionUnspecified` is the Go zero value (the default, `done`) and Flutter's `unspecified` is `TextInputActionPlatformDefault`; `TextInputType` is a comparable struct whose zero value is `text`; `TextSelection`, `TextEditingValue`, `TextInputConfiguration` and `AutofillConfiguration` have `Equal` and `CopyWith(XCopy)` | §12.1 and §12.3 |
| `FilteringTextInputFormatter` takes a `Pattern` (a `String` or a `RegExp`); `LengthLimitingTextInputFormatter(int? maxLength)` | the pattern is a `*regexp.Regexp` (RE2 syntax, UTF-8; a `String` pattern is `regexp.MustCompile(regexp.QuoteMeta(s))`), `FilteringTextInputFormatterSingleLine` and `FilteringTextInputFormatterDigitsOnly` are variables; `NewLengthLimitingTextInputFormatter(foundation.Opt[int])`; characters are grapheme clusters from go-text's segmenter; a limit of 0 or below -1 panics in a debug build with `MaxLengthInvalid`; the ported tests with non-ASCII text use byte offsets | Go has no `Pattern`; §12.10 text offsets |
| `TextEditingDelta.fromJSON` counts a deleted character as one UTF-16 unit; `TextLayoutMetrics.isWhitespace(int codeUnit)` | `NewTextEditingDeltaFromReplacement` counts a deleted character as the bytes of one UTF-8 character, so that the last character of the composing region goes whole; `TextLayoutMetricsIsWhitespace(rune)` takes a code point | UTF-8 offsets |
| The text input of a platform can be a `TextInputControl` of the app; `TextInputType.none` asks the platform for no on-screen keyboard | the platform control keeps the input method of the platform off while a control of the app is current and for `TextInputType.none` | on the desktop the input method is the keyboard |
| `ScribbleClient` and `TextInput.registerScribbleElement` (stylus handwriting on iOS); `SystemContextMenuController` (iOS) | not ported | no platform of Glutter has them |
| `AutofillScopeMixin.attach` sends the configurations of all the fields of the scope | `AutofillScopeAttach(scope, textInput, trigger, configuration)` checks that every field enables autofill (`AutofillClientDisabled` in a debug build) and attaches the trigger | no platform of Glutter fills fields |
| Wayland `zwp_text_input_v3` is not part of Flutter (the GTK embedder uses `GtkIMContext`) | `internal/platform/linux/wayland` binds the manager (version 1) when the compositor offers it: `enable`, surrounding text (at most 4000 bytes around the cursor), content type with the preedit-shown hint, cursor rectangle and `commit` follow `SetTextInput`; `done` delivers one `ui.TextInputEvent`; `leave` removes the preedit. The actions (`action`) are not used | §8.6 |
| IBus and Fcitx5 are reached through `GtkIMContext` and its input-method modules (the GTK embedder) | `internal/dbus` talks to them directly (`InputMethod`, `OpenInputMethod`): IBus on the private bus of its daemon (`IBUS_ADDRESS`, or the file `ibus/bus/<machine-id>-<host>-<display>` of the config directory) and, when that bus cannot be reached, through its portal `org.freedesktop.portal.IBus` on the session bus; Fcitx5 on `org.fcitx.Fcitx5` or `org.freedesktop.portal.Fcitx`. The framework named by `XMODIFIERS`, `GTK_IM_MODULE` or `QT_IM_MODULE` goes first; with no name, Fcitx5, then IBus. `ForwardKeyEvent` (IBus) and `ForwardKey` (Fcitx5), the content hints and the Fcitx5 cursor scale are not used. The same code serves X11 and Wayland without `zwp_text_input_v3` | SPEC §8.3 names only `org.freedesktop.IBus`, which a sandboxed app cannot reach; Gio reads the same file |
| `GtkIMContext` gets a key event and answers synchronously | while a text field has the focus every key goes to the input method first, on a goroutine of its own (`internal/platform/linux/ime`), with a limit of one second per call; the keys the input method does not consume are sent on in their order, and the key-up of a consumed key is dropped. On Wayland the key is translated (and the compose state advanced) before the input method answers | the UI thread must not wait for D-Bus (SPEC §13) |

| `RenderEditable` | `rendering.NewRenderEditable(textDirection, startHandleLayerLink, endHandleLayerLink, offset, textSelectionDelegate, opts)`; `maxLines` is `Opt[int]` with `MaxLinesUnlimited` (0) for Dart's `null` (the default of an unset value is 1, which an `Opt` could not tell from `null`), and the engine and the gesture binding are options (`TextEngine`, `Gestures`) because there is no global binding. The floating cursor of iOS, the Apple rules of `getLineAtOffset`-based selection and of the caret height, and the word selection that skips whitespace on iOS and Android are not ported. The asserts are the tags `EditableCursorColorMissing`, `EditableCursorHeightInvalid`, `EditableCursorWidthInvalid`, `EditableExpandsWithLines`, `EditableMaxLinesInvalid`, `EditableMinLinesGreaterThanMaxLines`, `EditableMinLinesInvalid`, `EditableNoTapDown`, `EditableObscuringCharacterInvalid`, `EditableWordOffsetNegative`, `EditableRecognizerNotReadOnly`, `EditableRecognizerUnsupported`, `EditableSemanticsInfoMissing` and `VerticalCaretMovementRunInvalid`; the assert of `_lineNumberFor` (no line for a position) is dropped because no position gets there | the Apple behaviors come with the Apple platforms; the others follow from the API conventions |
| `Paragraph.getLineBoundary`, `getGlyphInfoAt`, `getPositionForOffset` and `getBoxesForRange` at a line break | as Skia does: the line break that ends a line is not part of its `getLineBoundary` range; `getGlyphInfoAt` of the line break is a glyph without width at the end of the text of its line (or at the start of an empty line); `getBoxesForRange` of the line break alone is one box without width there (a range with more text has no box for it); the position at the end of the text of any line but the last is upstream, before the line break, as for a soft break. The caret before a line break and the vertical movement across lines need them | the editable text needs the caret at the end of every line |
| Grapheme clusters of the text | `foundation.GraphemeCount`, `GraphemeOffset` and `GraphemeBoundaries` and the paragraph layout use go-text's segmenter, plus a fix: v0.3.4 ends an emoji ZWJ sequence when another one follows it directly, so a cluster that ends with a ZWJ after a pictograph is joined to the next cluster that starts with a pictograph (rule GB11). The pictographs are an approximation of the Unicode `Extended_Pictographic` property (the emoji blocks and the symbols with an emoji presentation). All the offsets of the text editing are UTF-8 bytes | Dart's `characters` package is not available, and the fix goes when go-text fixes the segmenter |
| `EditableText`, `EditableTextState`, `TextEditingController` | `NewEditableText(controller, focusNode, style, cursorColor, backgroundCursorColor, opts)`; `maxLines` has the default 1 and `MaxLines: Some(0)` is `null`; the options with a default of `true` are negated. `TextEditingController` is a struct with `SetText`, `SetValue`, `SetSelection`, `Clear` and `ClearComposing` (Dart's setters), and `SetBuildTextSpan(f)` replaces Dart's override of `buildTextSpan`. The state implements `TextInputClient`, `TextSelectionDelegate`, `TextLayoutMetrics`'s delegate and `AutofillClient` and has the actions of the text editing intents (movement by character, word, line, paragraph, page and document; deletion; select all; copy, cut and paste through the clipboard; transposing characters; undo and redo through `UndoHistory`). The caret blinks (`cursorOpacityAnimates` does not fade it); the clipboard is read and written through callbacks, so a failure is reported with the tags `EditableTextClipboardFailed` and `EditableTextPasteFailed`. The failures of the constructor are the tags `EditableTextObscuringCharacterInvalid`, `EditableTextMinLinesInvalid`, `EditableTextMinLinesGreaterThanMaxLines`, `EditableTextExpandsWithLines` and `EditableTextObscuredMultiline`; `EditableTextNotBuilt` and `EditableTextNotMounted` guard the render object, `UpdateSelectionOutOfRange` the selection intent and `EditableTextTapOutsideTrackpad` a trackpad tap on a mobile platform; the controller has `TextEditingControllerSelectionInvalid` and `TextEditingControllerComposingInvalid`. Not ported: the stylus handwriting, Live Text, the processing of text by other apps, the floating cursor, the look up, search web and share buttons, `contentInsertionConfiguration` and the autofill groups beyond the hints `EditableTextOpts.SpellCheckConfiguration` and the state's spell check are ported (see the rows `SpellCheckConfiguration` and `EditableTextState.spellCheckResults`). | a Go controller cannot be subclassed; the rest follows from the platforms Glutter has |
| The composing text of the input method | the preedit of the input method (`TextInputEvent.Preedit`) is the composing range of the value and is underlined by the default `buildTextSpan`; the underline is cleared when the field loses the focus. A composing range that is not valid for the text panics with `TextEditingControllerComposingInvalid` in debug builds, like Flutter's assert | |
| `UndoHistory`, `UndoHistoryController`, `UndoManager` | `NewUndoHistory[T](value, onTriggered, focusNode, child, opts)` and `NewUndoHistoryController()`; the iOS `UndoManager` channel (`UndoManager.client`, `handlePlatformUndo`, `setUndoState`) is not ported. Undoing before the value was set panics with the tag `UndoHistoryValueNotSet` in debug builds | no platform has the iOS undo manager |
| `TapRegion`, `TapRegionSurface`, `TextFieldTapRegion`, `TapRegionRegistry` | `NewTapRegion(child, opts)` (`Disabled` is the negated `enabled`), `NewTapRegionSurface(child)` (a `WidgetsApp` has one) and `NewTextFieldTapRegion`; the callbacks take `*gestures.PointerDownEvent` and `*gestures.PointerUpEvent`; the surface does not take the semantics actions for a tap or a long press outside of a region. `TapRegionRegistryOf` without a surface panics with the tag `TapRegionRegistryMissing` in debug builds | the semantics actions are not wired to the surface yet |
| `TextSelectionGestureDetector` and `TextSelectionGestureDetectorBuilder` | the builder is a struct to embed (`InitTextSelectionGestureDetectorBuilder(b, self, delegate)`; `self` implements `TextSelectionGestureHandler`, the interface of the callbacks, so that the embedding builder overrides them); it takes the `TextSelectionGestureDetectorBuilderDelegate` of the field. Not ported: the force press, the stylus handwriting, the floating cursor; on Android and iOS the long press, the drag of the cursor and the double tap drag show and hide the magnifier of the field; the toolbar and the handles come with step 11.5. Triple taps select a line on Linux On Android a tap shows the spell check suggestions toolbar when the cursor is in a misspelled word; on iOS a touch tap on a misspelled word selects it and shows the toolbar, the next taps toggle it. | the mobile features need mobile platforms |
| `ClipboardStatusNotifier` | `NewClipboardStatusNotifier(binding)` takes the binding (no `WidgetsBinding.instance`) and the clipboard check is a callback; its failure is reported with the tag `ClipboardStatusCheckFailed` and the status becomes unknown | no global binding |
| `CharacterBoundary`, `LineBoundary`, `ParagraphBoundary` and `DocumentBoundary` | they are in `services` since step 11.4 and implement `TextBoundary`, which has `GetTextBoundaryAt`, `GetLeadingTextBoundaryAt` and `GetTrailingTextBoundaryAt`; the results that Dart returns as `int?` are a second `bool`; offsets are UTF-8 bytes and the characters are grapheme clusters | the Go API conventions |
| `CompositedTransformTarget` and `CompositedTransformFollower` | `NewCompositedTransformTarget(link, child)` and `NewCompositedTransformFollower(link, child, opts)`; the options with a default of `true` are negated (`NoShowWhenUnlinked`) | the options struct convention |
| `ContextMenuController` and `ContextMenuButtonItem`; `SystemContextMenu` | `NewContextMenuController(opts)` keeps the shown menu in the `WidgetsBinding` of the context that shows it, not in static fields; Dart's static `removeAny` is `ContextMenuControllerRemoveAny(bc)`. `ContextMenuButtonItem.Equal` compares the type, the label and whether there is a callback (Go cannot compare functions). `MarkNeedsBuild` on a menu that is not shown panics with `ContextMenuNotShown`. `SystemContextMenu` (iOS) and the web context menu are not ported | no global state |
| `TextSelectionOverlay`, `SelectionOverlay`, `TextSelectionControls`, `TextSelectionToolbarAnchors`, `TextSelectionHandleControls` | `TextSelectionControls` is an interface of the handle builder, anchor and size; every controls value is Flutter's `TextSelectionHandleControls` (the context menu builder makes the toolbar), so the deprecated `buildToolbar`, `canCut`, `canCopy`, `canPaste`, `canSelectAll` and the `clipboardStatus` and `toolbarLocation` parameters are not ported. `EmptyTextSelectionControls` builds nothing. `TextSelectionOverlay.ShowToolbar` during the build or layout panics with `ShowToolbarDuringBuild`. `TextSelectionOverlay.ShowSpellCheckSuggestionsToolbar(builder)` (hides the handles), `SpellCheckToolbarIsVisible()` and `SelectionOverlay.ShowSpellCheckSuggestionsToolbar(bc, builder)` show the spell check toolbar with a second `ContextMenuController`; `ToolbarIsVisible` includes it. `SelectionOverlayOpts` and `TextSelectionOverlayOpts` have `MagnifierConfiguration` (nil is disabled), and the overlays have `ShowMagnifier`, `UpdateMagnifier`, `HideMagnifier`, `MagnifierIsVisible` and `MagnifierExists`. | the deprecated path and the mobile extras |
| `SuggestionSpan`, `SpellCheckResults`, `SpellCheckService`, `DefaultSpellCheckService` | `SuggestionSpan{Range, Suggestions}` and `SpellCheckResults` have `Equal` and `String`; `SpellCheckService.FetchSpellCheckSuggestions(ctx, locale, text, done)` calls `done(spans, ok, err)` on the UI thread, never before it returns (`ok` false is Dart's null: a canceled request); `NewDefaultSpellCheckService(backend)` takes a `SpellCheckBackend` (`*ui.PlatformDispatcher` is one) and calls `ui.PlatformDispatcher.SpellCheck`, with `LastSavedResults()`, `DefaultSpellCheckServiceMergeResults` and `DefaultSpellCheckServiceSupportsSpellCheck(backend)`. A request that fails gives no result, as Flutter's catch returns null; a service without a backend fails with `SpellCheckServiceUnavailable`. Ranges are UTF-8 bytes | futures become callbacks; the flutter/spellcheck channel becomes the dispatcher (§24.6); text offsets are bytes |
| `nativeSpellCheckServiceDefined`, `DefaultSpellCheckService.supportsSpellCheck` | true only when `foundation.DefaultTargetPlatform()` is Android and `ui.PlatformDispatcher.SpellCheckSupported()` is true (the host has `ui.SpellCheckHost`), so Linux and the web keep spell check off, as in Flutter; iOS and macOS have no spell checker in Glutter | Glutter's spell checker is Android's `TextServicesManager` only |
| `SpellCheckConfiguration`, `SpellCheckConfiguration.disabled()` | `NewSpellCheckConfiguration(SpellCheckConfigurationOpts{SpellCheckService, MisspelledSelectionColor (Opt), MisspelledTextStyle, SpellCheckSuggestionsToolbarBuilder})`, `NewSpellCheckConfigurationDisabled()`, `CopyWith(SpellCheckConfigurationCopy)` (a disabled configuration stays disabled); a nil `*SpellCheckConfiguration` is Dart's null. `Equal` compares the toolbar builders by the code they run, since Go cannot compare functions | no function equality |

Flutter's `_BodyBoxConstraints` (the body builder reads the heights of the bottom widgets and the app bar from the constraints) is `LayoutBuilderOpts.Extra` (below). A floating snack bar that does not fit panics with `FloatingSnackBarOffScreen`. The drawers (`Drawer`, `EndDrawer`, `OnDrawerChanged`, `OnEndDrawerChanged`, `DrawerScrimColor`, `NoDrawerBarrierDismissible`, `DrawerEdgeDragWidth`, `NoDrawerEnableOpenDragGesture`, `NoEndDrawerEnableOpenDragGesture`, `DrawerDragStartBehavior`), `OpenDrawer`, `OpenEndDrawer`, `CloseDrawer`, `CloseEndDrawer`, `IsDrawerOpen`, `IsEndDrawerOpen`, `IsDrawerBarrierDismissible`, the persistent bottom sheet (`ScaffoldOpts.BottomSheet`), `ScaffoldState.ShowBottomSheet` and the function `ShowBottomSheet(bc, builder, ShowBottomSheetOpts)` (it returns a `*PersistentBottomSheetController`, which embeds the `ScaffoldFeatureController`; the tags `ScaffoldBottomSheetShowConflict` and `ScaffoldBottomSheetUpdateConflict` are the assertions about `Scaffold.bottomSheet` together with a sheet that `ShowBottomSheet` made), the body scrim (`BottomSheetScrimBuilder`, `ShowBodyScrim`) and the `DismissIntent` (Escape) action that closes the drawers are in. Not part of it: the status bar tap target (a Linux desktop has no status bar to tap), the hiding of the floating action button and the scrim while a `DraggableScrollableSheet` is dragged (the sheet and its notifications are not ported), the history entry that a persistent sheet adds when it is dragged up, `restorationId` (no state restoration, §20). The floating action button has its locations (`FloatingActionButtonLocationEndFloat` and the other eighteen, which are `StandardFabLocation` values; an app can write its own with `GetOffset(ScaffoldPrelayoutGeometry)`), `ScaffoldOpts.FloatingActionButtonAnimator` (`FloatingActionButtonAnimatorScaling`, the default, and `FloatingActionButtonAnimatorNoAnimation`), the move to a new location with the animation of `_moveFloatingActionButton`, and the transition that scales and turns the button when it changes (`_FloatingActionButtonTransition`). `ScaffoldState.showFloatingActionButton` shows the button again when a bottom sheet closes | the options struct convention; Go has no constraint subclass; what depends on `DraggableScrollableSheet` is not ported |
| `LayoutBuilder` with extra layout information | `LayoutBuilderOpts.Extra func() any`: called at layout time; the builder runs again when its value (a comparable one) differs from the one of the last build, though the constraints did not change. `SliverLayoutBuilder` is built the same way | the `BoxConstraints` of Go has no subclass to carry the extra fields of `_BodyBoxConstraints` |
| `AppBar`, `AppBarThemeData`, `AppBarTheme` | `NewAppBar(AppBarOpts{...})` has every option of Flutter's (the negated booleans are `NoAutomaticallyImplyLeading`, `NoAutomaticallyImplyActions`, `NoPrimary`, `NoUseDefaultSemanticsOrder`); Material 3 and Material 2 defaults (`appBarDefaultsM3`, `appBarDefaultsM2`); the toolbar is 56 high (`ToolbarHeight`), as Flutter's `kToolbarHeight` (step 12.5 had 64); the title box `_AppBarTitleBox`, the scroll-under elevation by a `ScrollNotificationObserver`, `AppBarPreferredHeightFor(bc, w)`. `NewAppBarTheme(data, child)` takes the one `AppBarThemeData` (Flutter's `AppBarTheme` constructor with the same fields as the data is the deprecated one), `AppBarThemeOf(bc)` falls back to `ThemeData.AppBarTheme`; `AppBarThemeDataLerp`, `Equal`, `CopyWith`. The leading button is a `DrawerButton`, a `BackButton` or a `CloseButton` (`automaticallyImplyLeading`), and in Material 3 an `IconButton` leading is centered in an `IconButtonTheme` that has the icon theme of the bar; the actions get an `IconButtonTheme` with the actions icon theme. The system overlay style is given in an `AnnotatedRegion[SystemUiOverlayStyle]` that nothing reads yet (there is no `SystemChrome`) | the options struct convention; the deprecated parts of Flutter are not ported |
| `SliverAppBar`, `SliverAppBar.medium`, `SliverAppBar.large` | `NewSliverAppBar`, `NewSliverAppBarMedium`, `NewSliverAppBarLarge(SliverAppBarOpts{...})`; `Pinned` is an `Opt[bool]` because its default is true for the medium and large ones; `StretchTriggerOffset` is an `Opt` (default 100) and `OnStretchTrigger` is a plain `func()` (SPEC §12.10, `OverScrollHeaderStretchConfiguration`). The debug assertions are the tags `SliverAppBarSnapNotFloating`, `SliverAppBarStretchTriggerOffset` and `SliverAppBarCollapsedHeight` | the options struct convention |

The tags are `FlexibleSpaceBarExpandedTitleScale`, `FlexibleSpaceBarNoSettings` and `FlexibleSpaceBarSettingsInvalid` | enum convention |

The reason arguments are variadic (`HideCurrentSnackBar(SnackBarClosedReasonTimeout)`; the default is hide, remove for the `Remove…` ones). The timer of the snack bar is `GestureBinding.NewTimer`, so it follows the fake clock in tests. The tags `ShowSnackBarNoScaffold` and `ShowMaterialBannerNoScaffold` are the assertions that there is a scaffold to show in | no futures (§12.1) |

The snack bar is not wrapped in a `Dismissible` (`widgets/dismissible.dart` is not ported; `DismissDirection` is only the enum, and swiping does nothing; the semantics dismiss action works), the action is a `TextButton` (with the style of `TextButtonStyleFrom` and a `TextButtonTheme` for the colors of the states) and the close icon an `IconButton`. A property in the states of the button is a `WidgetStateProperty`: of `foundation.Opt[T]` for the colors, the doubles, the sizes and the border side (`Opt` none is a `null` value, which leaves the choice to the next style) and of the type itself for the types that have a nil (`*TextStyle`, `EdgeInsetsGeometry`, `OutlinedBorder`, `MouseCursor`). `CopyWith(overrides)` keeps the property that the argument leaves nil; `Merge(style)` keeps the ones of the receiver, as Flutter's `merge`; `Equal` compares a property that is a `WidgetStatePropertyAll` or a map by value; `ButtonStyleLerp(a, b, t)`. `IconAlignment` has the zero value `IconAlignmentUnspecified` (the start) | the options struct convention; Dart's nullable property |

The `.icon` constructors are `NewTextButtonIcon(onPressed, label, TextButtonIconOpts{ButtonStyleButtonOpts, Icon, IconAlignment})` (and `NewElevatedButtonIcon`, `NewFilledButtonIcon`, `NewFilledButtonTonalIcon`, `NewOutlinedButtonIcon`, whose options are aliases of it): a nil `Icon` gives the label alone, as `icon: null` does. The `iconAlignment` argument of `ButtonStyleButton`, which Flutter ignores and has deprecated, is not ported. `ButtonStyleButton` is a struct that the four buttons embed; the interface `ButtonStyleButtonSpec` (`ButtonStyleButtonBase`, `DefaultStyleOf(bc)`, `ThemeStyleOf(bc)`) is what its state reads, since Go has no abstract methods. `styleFrom` is `TextButtonStyleFrom(ButtonStyleFromOpts)`, `ElevatedButtonStyleFrom`, `FilledButtonStyleFrom`, `OutlinedButtonStyleFrom`: all four take the one `ButtonStyleFromOpts` (the properties that they have in common; each function keeps the differences of the Flutter one: the disabled color of the text and outlined buttons, the elevations of the states of the elevated one). The static helpers are `ButtonStyleButtonAllOrNull`, `ButtonStyleButtonDefaultColor` and `ButtonStyleButtonScaledPadding`. The defaults of Material 3 (`_TextButtonDefaultsM3`...) are built when the style is asked for (Flutter's getters build each property when it is read). A Material 3 button is a `ButtonStyleButton` (`iconButtonM3`) whose defaults depend on the variant and on whether it toggles; the button of Material 2 is an `InkResponse`. **Deviation:** Dart's `identical(iconTheme.color, kDefaultIconDarkColor)` (the icon theme still has the default color) compares the colors by value. | `FloatingActionButtonLocation`, `StandardFabLocation`, `FabTopOffsetY`..., `FloatingActionButtonAnimator`, `kFloatingActionButtonMargin`, `kFloatingActionButtonSegue`, `kFloatingActionButtonTurnInterval`, `kMiniButtonOffsetAdjustment` | `FloatingActionButtonLocation` is an interface (`GetOffset(ScaffoldPrelayoutGeometry)`) and each static of Flutter is a package level value: `FloatingActionButtonLocationStartTop`, `…MiniStartTop`, `…CenterTop`, `…EndTop`, `…StartFloat`, `…EndFloat`, `…StartDocked`, `…EndDocked`, `…EndContained` and the others of the table, which are `*fabLocation` with a `String()` of `FloatingActionButtonLocation.endFloat`. `StandardFabLocation` is an interface (`GetOffsetX`, `GetOffsetY`, `IsMini`) with `StandardFabLocationOffset(l, geometry)`, and the mixins of Flutter (`FabTopOffsetY`, `FabFloatOffsetY`, `FabDockedOffsetY`, `FabContainedOffsetY`, `FabStartOffsetX`, `FabCenterOffsetX`, `FabEndOffsetX`) are functions that an app composes. `FloatingActionButtonAnimator` is an interface (`GetOffset`, `GetScaleAnimation`, `GetRotationAnimation`, `GetAnimationRestart`) with the values `FloatingActionButtonAnimatorScaling` and `FloatingActionButtonAnimatorNoAnimation`; the constants are `FloatingActionButtonMargin`, `FloatingActionButtonSegue`, `FloatingActionButtonTurnInterval` and `MiniButtonOffsetAdjustment`. | Window decorations (Flutter's GTK embedder draws them with GTK) | the choice is made per window, in `internal/platform/linux/wayland/decoration.go`: `DecorationServerSide` when the compositor has `zxdg_decoration_manager_v1`, else `DecorationLibdecor` when `libdecor-0.so.0` loads and starts, else `DecorationApp`; `WindowConfig.Decorations = DecorationsApp` and `Decorated: false` skip it (`DecorationApp`, `DecorationNone`). The platform reports it, with the title, the `wm_capabilities`, and the maximized, fullscreen, active and tiled states, in `ViewMetrics.Window` (`ui.WindowInfo`, read with `View.Window()`). A compositor that answers a request for server-side decorations with client-side ones turns the window into `DecorationApp`. `GLUTTER_DECORATIONS=auto\|app\|libdecor` chooses for the first window (`libdecor` is a test aid: libdecor even where the compositor has its own bars; the error tag of any other value is `DecorationsUnknown`). A libdecor window has no `xdg_surface` and no `xdg_toplevel` of its own (libdecor makes them), so the window goes through `decor.Frame` for the title, the size limits, the states, moves and resizes, and answers a configure with `libdecor_frame_commit`; libdecor's file descriptor is a source of the loop and `Dispatch` runs when it is readable. libdecor's own tags are `LibdecorInitFailed`, `LibdecorDecorateFailed` and `LibdecorDispatchFailed`. Without a plugin libdecor starts with a dummy one and the window has no title bar; Glutter cannot tell | SPEC §8.2, D16 |
| `WindowDecoration` | `NewWindowDecoration(child, WindowDecorationOpts{...})` in package `widgets`: the bar (title, minimize, maximize or restore, close, limited by the capabilities), a drag of it moves the window, a double tap maximizes or restores it, a right click opens the window menu, and strips of 6 pixels along the edges start a resize; it shows the child alone when another decoration draws the title bar. `RunApp` wraps the app in one unless `WindowConfig.Decorations` is `DecorationsApp`. The child keeps its state when the bar appears (a global key), since the compositor says the mode after the first frame. The actions go through `View.PerformWindowAction` and `ui.WindowHost` (the engine) to `platform.Window.PerformWindowAction`, which needs the serial of the last pointer press | the title bar of the GTK embedder is not Flutter API |
| Shadow of `WindowDecoration` | the bar has a one pixel outline and no drop shadow, so `xdg_surface.set_window_geometry` is not used for it (SPEC §8.2 says the shadow and the resize borders sit inside the surface and are excluded with it) | no step of the plan takes the shadow: it needs the geometry of the surface, and the resize borders and the input region with it |

They fail with `WindowingUnsupported` (the platform cannot) or the platform's error, and panic with `WindowSizeViolatesConstraints` in debug builds (Flutter's `assert`); a dialog whose parent was destroyed fails with `WindowParentDestroyed`. Windowing is always on | no singletons (§13); Go returns errors |

| `activate()` | asks the platform; on Wayland `xdg_toplevel` cannot raise a window or take the focus for it, so it asks `xdg_activation_v1` for a token (with the serial of the last input event) and activates the window with it, and the compositor may only mark the window as wanting attention; without the protocol it does nothing. X11 sends `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` | the protocol (step 14.7) |
| the window controllers cannot hide a window: closing is destroying, and closing the last window ends the app | `RegularWindowController.SetHidden(hidden)` and `IsHidden()` (and `Activate` shows a hidden window first), through `ui.PlatformDispatcher.SetWindowHidden` (`ui.WindowControlHost`, the engine) and `platform.Window.SetHidden(hidden) bool`: Wayland unmaps the surface and makes a new role to show it (§8.2), X11 withdraws the window (§8.3), the web ignores it (§23.2). A hidden window still counts as open (`RunApp` goes on), keeps its view and widget state, and when every window is hidden the lifecycle is `Hidden` (§13). Not on `DialogWindowController` | an app that lives in the system tray closes its window to the tray (§8.7); Flutter's desktop apps do it with a plugin (`window_manager`) |
| a window has no icon and no content type API; Flutter's apps get the icon of the platform's runner | `WindowConfig.Icon`, `WindowConfig.ContentType`, the options `Icon` and `ContentType` of both window controllers, and `SetIcon(icon) error` and `SetContentType(t)` on them (and on `ui.View`): `ui.WindowIcon{Name, Images}` (square RGBA pictures, `ui.WindowIconImage{Size, Scale, RGBA}`; `WindowIconInvalid` for a wrong picture) and `ui.ContentType` (`ContentTypeUnspecified`, `Photo`, `Video`, `Game`). The host side is `ui.WindowAppearanceHost`, which the engine implements with `platform.Window.SetIcon` and `SetContentType`. Wayland uses `xdg_toplevel_icon_v1` and `wp_content_type_v1`; X11 uses `_NET_WM_ICON` for the pictures and has no content type | the desktop's icon and the compositor's hints (SPEC §8.7, §8.11) |

| `flutter_test` has no windows | `Tester.Windows()` lists the windows the app created with the controllers, not the implicit view, so that `Windows()[0]` stays the first window the app made; `Tester.ViewTitle(view)` is the last title set for any view, the implicit one included, and the state of the implicit window is `ViewActions` and `SetWindowInfo` as before | the existing tests index `Windows()` from the first created window (step 27.4) |

The keys, the text input and the cursor still follow the keyboard: a platform whose keyboard focus can differ from its window focus (Wayland) also calls the optional `platform.KeyboardFocusHandler.OnKeyboardFocus`. The engine queues the event with the input of the next frame, and asks the platform to activate a window when the framework moves the focus into it.
