The Java side reports `AccessibilityManager`'s state (`Native.a11yState(enabled, touchExploration, accessibleNavigation)`, once at the start and on each change); Go turns the framework's tree on while a service runs or touch exploration is on, and sends command 18 (reset) when it turns off. `accessibleNavigation` follows Flutter (set by the first `createAccessibilityNodeInfo`, cleared when the service or touch exploration goes) and is also set when touch exploration turns on; it reaches `ui.AccessibilityFeatures` through `platform.Settings.AccessibleNavigation`. Each `ui.SemanticsUpdate` becomes one payload (command 16; the layout is the comment of `accessibility_wire.go`, mirrored in `GlutterAccessibility.java`, with the golden bytes in both). Go works out everything Android needs of a node (`newAndroidNode`: class name, properties, texts, actions, range and collection shape; the class names follow the order of Flutter's role configurators) and converts the offsets to UTF-16; `GlutterAccessibility` keeps the tree on the main thread and does the geometry (as `updateRecursively`), the hit test, the focus-dependent parts, the predicted state (cursor moves, set text, set selection, increase and decrease), the events and `performAction`. Actions come back as `Native.a11yAction(node, ui.SemanticsAction bit, args)` with the offsets already in bytes. While the bridge is off, updates are dropped. A new activity in a living process turns the bridge off and on, so the framework sends the whole tree again. In debug builds `GLUTTER_A11Y=1` makes Go send command 17, which forces the bridge on, so that the images without TalkBack can test it; the events are then only logged, because `AccessibilityManager` throws while no service runs (with a service, such as `uiautomator`, they are sent). Every event is logged at Info with the tag `GlutterJava` as `a11y event type=<NAME> node=<id> text=<text>` (with ` live=1` for a live region's change and ` pane=1` for a route's pane title), and so are `a11y bridge on|off`, `a11y tree nodes=<n>` and `a11y dev action <name> node=<id> label=<label> result=<bool>`. The dev events script has `a11y:<action>,<label>` with the action one of `click`, `longclick`, `scrollforward`, `scrollbackward`, `focus`, `a11yfocus`, `a11yclear`, `dismiss`, `copy`, `cut`, `paste`, `expand` and `collapse`: the first node whose text, content description or hint contains the label and that offers the action (else the first node with the label, then the first node below it that offers the action) gets `performAction`, as TalkBack does. Not done: platform views, `SemanticsService` announcements and tooltips, text traversal by line, paragraph and page (taken, and the cursor predicted, but nothing is sent to the framework), extra data for node infos, `stateDescription`. The device tests use `examples/dev/android-a11y` and `uiautomator dump`, which reads the screen through an accessibility service as TalkBack does.

### 24.11 Services

- `desktop.OpenURI` starts an `ACTION_VIEW` intent for the URI, after the same validation as on Linux; no app for the URI is `OpenURIUnavailable`.
- `desktop.ShowNotification` posts through `NotificationManager`, on one channel per urgency. On Android 13 and later it asks for `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` once per decision, as the web asks for its permission (§23.7); a refusal is `NotificationsUnavailable`. Actions are buttons, a click is the action `"default"`, and the closes are reported as Android reports them.
- The tray is `TrayUnavailable`. The file dialogs are `FileChooserUnavailable` in 1.6: the Storage Access Framework gives `content:` URIs, not paths, so it needs its own API (a later decision, §21). D-Bus is never tried on Android.

**Decided in step 25.12.** *Where it lives.* `internal/platform/mobile.Desktop` is the Android side of the desktop services as plain Go (the twin of the web's `Desktop`, §23.7) over two small interfaces, `IntentOpener` and `NotificationAPI`; it has its own package, without an OS name, so that a Linux or web binary that imports `desktop` takes no Android backend (`TestAppsWithDesktopServicesImportNoForeignBackend` next to `TestBuildsHaveTheirOwnBackends`). `internal/platform/android/desktop.go` (plain Go) is the link to `DesktopServices.java` through the commands 19 to 23 of `GlutterActivity.command` (19 open, 20 notification status, 21 permission request, 22 notify, 23 cancel; each that needs an answer carries a request number) and the natives `Native.desktopResult(request, code, text)` and `Native.notificationEvent(serial, kind, text)` (click, button, swipe), which hand a value to a waiting goroutine and never wait for the UI thread. The platform offers it as `platform.DesktopProvider`, the engine as `ui.PlatformDispatcher.PlatformService("desktop")`, and package `desktop` takes it in `servicesOf` instead of asking the target platform (tests can override that); with it set, `services.connect` fails with `DBusUnavailableOnAndroid`, so the tray and the file chooser end in their own tags and D-Bus is never tried. *Open.* `OpenURI` validates as on Linux and the web (`javascript:` and `vbscript:` are `OpenURISchemeBlocked`) and calls `startActivity(ACTION_VIEW)` on the shown activity; `ActivityNotFoundException`, or no activity, is `OpenURIUnavailable` (Android 10 and later can refuse to start an activity of an app that is not on the screen, which is not detected). *Notifications.* One channel per urgency; the body click, the buttons and the swipe come back through `PendingIntent`s to the activity (`onNewIntent`, with a per-process stamp so that an intent left by an earlier process is ignored) and to `NotificationReceiver` (the delete intent); the same Android id replaces a notification, and the replaced one's events are ignored. The generated manifest always declares `android.permission.POST_NOTIFICATIONS` (the toolkit's own service needs it; `-permission` adds others) and the receiver. *Permission.* From API 33 the first `ShowNotification` requests `POST_NOTIFICATIONS` on the call's goroutine (not the UI's; requests are serialized and share one prompt), a refusal or `areNotificationsEnabled() == false` or a disabled channel is `NotificationsUnavailable`, and a request ended by its context is `NotificationPermissionAborted`. The device tests grant it with `pm grant`, and deny it with `pm revoke` and `pm set-permission-flags user-fixed`, which makes the system answer without a dialog. Logs: the Java side logs `desktop open|status|permission|notify|cancel|notification event ...` with the tag `GlutterJava`. Not supported: notification icons, inline reply, more than three buttons, delivery of a tap on a notification of an earlier process, replacing across a process restart.

### 24.12 Framework behavior on Android

`foundation.DefaultTargetPlatform` is `TargetPlatformAndroid` on Android (`foundation/host_android.go`), so the framework already takes Flutter's Android branches: clamping scroll physics, the Android typography, the tap target sizes, and so on. The Android parts that earlier steps left out are ported in M26:
- `StretchingOverscrollIndicator` and `GlowingOverscrollIndicator`, and `MaterialScrollBehavior` building them (step 26.1)
- the Material `TextSelectionToolbar` with its overflow menu, `TextSelectionToolbarTextButton`, the Android branch of `AdaptiveTextSelectionToolbar`, and `TextMagnifier` (step 26.2)
- `PredictiveBackPageTransitionsBuilder` and the Android default of `PageTransitionsTheme` at the pinned commit (step 26.3)
- spell check: `SpellCheckConfiguration`, `SpellCheckSuggestionsToolbar` and the Android service (§24.6)
InkSparkle stays a deviation (it needs a custom fragment shader, a non-goal, §2), and so does Cupertino.

### 24.13 Testing and proof

- **Standard checks** (from step 25.1 on): `scripts/check-android.sh` builds and vets the module for `android/arm64` and `android/amd64` with the NDK's clang. It needs only the NDK, no device. The plain-Go parts of the platform (event conversion, key tables, insets math, the manifest, the font matcher's parser) are in files without an OS tag and are tested in the normal run.
- **Device tests** (`-tags glutter_android`) run on the emulator: `internal/androidtest` creates the virtual devices `glutter-37` (`system-images;android-37.0;google_apis;x86_64`, the default), `glutter-37-16k` (`system-images;android-37.2;google_apis_ps16k;x86_64`, 16 KB pages) and `glutter-26` (`system-images;android-26;google_apis;x86_64`, the minimum API level, from step 25.7 on) in `.android-test/` at the module root (git-ignored, so the quick-boot snapshot survives between runs), starts the emulator headless (`-no-window -no-audio -no-boot-anim`, `-gpu host` or `swiftshader_indirect`) under `timeout -k` in its own process group with its own `adb` server port and `HOME`, installs a test APK, starts it, reads the results the app writes (through `adb exec-out run-as`) and its `logcat` lines, and stops the emulator and the `adb` server. Nothing of the user's `~/.android` is read or written.

**Decided in step 25.2.** `internal/androidtest` starts the emulator with `-no-window -no-audio -no-boot-anim -no-snapshot -wipe-data -no-metrics -gpu swangle_indirect -port <even port from 5584 down>` under `timeout -k 10s <lifetime>` in a process group of its own, with a private `adb` server on a free port (`ANDROID_ADB_SERVER_PORT`), `HOME`, `ANDROID_USER_HOME` and `ANDROID_AVD_HOME` inside `.android-test/`, and without `DISPLAY` and `WAYLAND_DISPLAY`. It writes the `config.ini` of each virtual device itself (a 1080x2400 phone at 420 dpi, hardware keyboard off) instead of calling `avdmanager`. `Stop` sends `adb emu kill`, kills the group after 20 seconds, stops the `adb` server, and `Leftovers` (which reads the environment of the processes in `/proc`) must list nothing; `androidtest.Run` fails the package's tests otherwise. **Findings on this machine (emulator 37.2.12):** `-gpu swiftshader_indirect` crashes the emulator (SIGSEGV after "emulatorSetupEnvironment"), `-gpu host` needs a display (`GlxEngine ... Failed to open display`), and `swangle_indirect` boots in about 20 seconds; the image `system-images;android-37.0;google_apis;x86_64` does not boot (the system server crashes in a loop in `AdvancedProtectionService`: "Invalid feature flag: android.security.aapm_feature_disable_insecure_wifi_autojoin"), so **`glutter-37` and `glutter-37-16k` both use `system-images;android-37.2;google_apis_ps16k;x86_64`** until the owner installs a newer revision of the 37.0 image (the device tests of API 26 keep 4 KB pages covered).
- **The device tests take longer than the default ten minutes of `go test`** (two virtual devices boot one after the other): run them with `-timeout 80m`.
- **Proof method E**: `glutter-android shot [-avd glutter-37] [-renderer r] [-env K=V]... [-screen] -o file.png <package>` builds and installs the app, starts it with `GLUTTER_SCREENSHOT=1`, and pulls the PNG the app saves after its first idle frame (step 7.4) from its files directory. `-screen` saves the device's whole screen with `adb exec-out screencap -p` instead, for what lies outside the app's surface: the status bar, the keyboard, a notification.
- **Debug-only scripted events.** In debug builds `GLUTTER_DEV_EVENTS` (an intent extra) is a script in the form of the web's (§23.4 "Decided in step 21.2"), replayed after the first frame by the Java side on the main thread, through the same paths as the user's input: `tap:x,y`, `swipe:x1,y1,x2,y2,ms`, `key:KEYCODE_X`, `text:...` and `compose:a,b,c` through the input connection, `back:start,progress...,commit|cancel` through the back callbacks, `a11y:action,label` through the accessibility provider, `wait:ms`. The device tests and the proofs use it where `adb shell input` cannot reach (the input connection, the back gesture, accessibility actions). A bad script is `AndroidDevEventsInvalid`.
- **Vulkan validation** on Android: the Khronos validation layer for Android (`libVkLayer_khronos_validation.so`, from the Android release of Vulkan-ValidationLayers) is packed into the test APKs by `glutter-android build -vulkan-validation <dir>`, and a validation error fails the device test.
- **Performance measurements** (step 26.5): `glutter-android perf [-avd glutter-37 | -device serial] [-release=true] [-abi arm64,amd64] [-renderer r] [-env K=V]... [-starts N] [-settle d] [-seconds N] [-format text|markdown] <package>` builds the package (release by default, signed with the debug key so that it installs over a debug build), installs it and prints the APK size per ABI and the size of each `libapp.so`, the cold start (`am start -W`'s TotalTime and WaitTime, and the engine's `first frame presented since_run`, over N starts), the idle CPU (`/proc/<pid>/stat` over the window), the engine's frame statistics (`GLUTTER_FRAME_STATS=1`, parsed by `internal/perfstats`, which `glutter-web perf` shares) and the memory (`dumpsys meminfo` PSS and RSS, and `VmRSS` and `VmHWM`). `GLUTTER_DEV_EVENTS` is read only in debug builds, so a scenario that taps or swipes needs `-release=false` (`perf` refuses the pair, `AndroidPerfDevEventsRelease`). `scripts/perf-android.sh` runs the counter, the gallery with an animation, the gallery rebuilt in every frame and the gallery navigation scenario, for the emulator or a phone (`-d serial`). The emulator's GPU is SwiftShader, so its raster times are not a phone's (§15).
- **Real phones.** The emulator runs x86_64 code and translates GLES and Vulkan to the host's GPU, so it cannot show arm64 code or a phone's GPU driver. The steps that touch input, rendering or text input end with manual checks on the owner's phone (`glutter-android run`), recorded in `IMPLEMENTED.md`.
